Ser-lll/2015 daled 17-02-2019, 18-02-2019 in pursuance of the orders issued in

of rights guaranteed under Articles 14, 16 and 2'1 of the Constitution of lndia,
apart from contrary to the statutory rules and also contrary to the orders of

upgraded posts of P.E.Ts as School Assistants with the existing P.E.Ts by

Petition under Article 226 of the Constitution of lndia praying that in the
circumstances stated in the affidavit filed therewith, the High Court may be
pleased to issue a Writ order or order more in the nature of Writ of [\,4andamus
declare the impugned action of the respondents herein in not considering
petitioners claim for promotion to the post o[ School Assistant (Language) which
are existing as on 04-02-2021 by applying the principle of Y.V.Rangaiahs case,
existed prior to the amended rules of Education Subordinate Service Rules for
the posts in lr,landal parishad and Zilla Parishad and Government Schools issued
in G.O.tr/s-No. 2 and 3 dt.05.02.2021 though rt is prospective effect and not filling
the said vacancies as per Rules rssued in G O.lr4s.No. 11 and 12 dt.23-01-2009
by combined seniority lists of S.G.Ts/Language Pandits as per the ratio law laid
down by Apex Court in Y.V.Rangaiah and others (1983 (3 SCC 284 and this
Hon'ble Court judgment in W.P.No 2328312017 and batch is illegal, unjust and
unwarranted and consequently declare that the petitioners are entitled to be
promoted as School Assistant ( La ng uages/Physical Education) as per pre-amended rules with all consequential benefits.

4. In W.l'.Nos.20307,21552 and 22326 of 2022, the petitioners are
chattenging G.O.Ms.No. 1 1 0, Finance (HRM-II) Department,
dt.05.10.202 I u,ithdrawing the orders issued in G.O.Ms.No.17 and
G.O.Ms.No.r8 dt.03.02.2017 with respect to upgradation ol certain
posts ol [,anguage Pandits and Physical Education Teacherr; as School
Assistants (L anguages) and (Physicat Education), as illegal and arbitrary
and ir.r violation ol the law laid down by the Flon'ble Apex Cour.t in the
case of Y.V.Rangaiah and others Vs. J.Sreenivasa Rao and others (1
supra).
5. Brief facts leading to filing of the present Writ Petitions are that
the petitioners are all working as Secondary Grade Teachers (SGTs).

Thus, it can be seen that the post of a School Assistant of any subject or
language could be filled up by direct recruitment or also by promotion
lrom Categories 1 to 25 of Class-II which includes Secondary Grade
Teachers at Category 1 in CIass-II, provided they possessed the
necessaly qualification. The qualifications for the said posts are also
given in Rule 5. The relevant poftion of Rule 5 is extracted as under:
' 5. Qualifications:
(i) No person shall be eligible for qppointment to the
cotegories specified in Column (3) of the Table by the
method speciJied in Column (2) unless he/she possesses
the qualifications specified in the coresponding enlry in
Column (4) thereof.

22. It is therelore necessary to examine whether the vacancies which
have arisen prior to the amendment of the Rules in the year 2021 ate lo
be filled up in accordance with the decision of the Hon'ble Supreme
Court in the r:ase of Y.V.Rangaiah and others Vs. J.Sreenivasa Rao
and others (1 supra). A Larger Bench of the Hon'ble Supreme Court in
the case of State of Himachal Pradesh and others Vs. Raj Kumar
and othcrs (2 supra), while considering the case of the writ petitioners

W P.No.10061/2017 & batch
(.fotal l4 Cascs)
3t
therein rvho were seeking promotion to the posts of Labour Otficers by
way of promotion as per old Ruies rvhich existed when the vacancies
arose or as per the subsequent amended Rules, has distinguished the
decision of Y.V.Rangaiah (l supra). In the said case, the brief facts are
that additional posts were sanctioned and thereafter, Rules came to be
amended under the New Rules called the H.P. Labour and Employment
Department, Labour Officers, Class-ll (Gazetted) Ministerial Service R
& P Rutcs, 2006 and recruitment to the post of Labour Officer was to be
made by promotion as well as direct recruitrnent in the ratio of 75 per
cent and 25 per cent respectively and the effect of New Rules coupled
with the 7 new posts for Labour Officers is that, from out of the total
number of 12 posts of Labour Officers, the promotional posts increased
from 5 to 9 (being 75 per cent) and direct recruitment posts came to 3
(being 25 per cent). Immediately thereafter, the Govemment issued a
Notification creating 12 Labour Zones in the State and ln such
background, the candidates approached the Administrative Tribunal of
the State challenging the proposed action of the State Govemment in
filling up 25 per cent of the posts of Labour Officers by direct
,l
recruitment. They contended that the vacancies arose in July, 2006,

W.l'.No. 1006I '1017 & batch
( lotal l-l Cajcs)
32
which is belorc the promulgation of the New Rules and therctbre, all the
vacancies nrust be filted up only by promotion. By its order
dt.24.01.2007. the Tribunal directed the State Govetnment to considcr
the grievance raised by the applicants as if it is a representation to it.
'I'he Governrnent thereafter rejected the same on 27 .06.2007. The mattcr
has rcached the Hon'ble Supreme Court and the Hon'ble Suprcme Court
afier considcring its various judgments both in consonance with its
dccision in the case of Y.V.Rangaiah and others Vs. J.Sreenivasa
Rao rnd others (1 supra) and also distinguishing the same. has come to
the conclusioti that Y.V.Rangaiah's case ( 1 supra) must be seen in the
context of its own facts, coupled with declarations therein that there is
no rule of universal application to the effect that vacrrncies tnust
necessarily be filled on the basis of the rules which existed on the date
on which they arose, and held that the decision in Y.V.Rangaiah's case
(1 supra) is impliedty ovemrled. The Hon'ble Supreme Clourt further
held that as there is no declaration of law to this effect, it continues to be
cited as a precedent and the Court has distinguished it on some ground
or the other and therefore, it was held that the decision in the case of
Y.V.Rangaiah and others Vs. J.Sreenivasa Rao and others (1 supra)

23. The latest decision of the Hon'ble Supreme Court in the case of
State of Himachal Pradcsh and others Vs. Raj Kumar anrl others (2

W.P.No. 1006 1/2017 & barch

37
supra) has considered all the decisions which have distinguished the
decision in the case of Y.V.Rangaiah and held that the principle laid
dou,n therein that the vacancies which occurred prior to the amended
Rules would be governed by the old Rules and not by amended Rules,
does not reflect the correct preposition of law and that it is ovem-rled.
What follows therelrom is that the rules in force as on the date of
consideration for promotion would apply.
21. In the cases before this Court, the posts of School Assistants
upgraded under G.O.Ms.No.17 and G.O.Ms.No.18, Finance ([IRM-II)
Depaftment, both dt.03.02.2017 were intended to be filled up by

The said G.Os., were withdrawn only vide G.O.Ms.No.l 10, Finance
(HRM-II) Department, dt.05.10.2021 and reistructured to be filled up as
per thg amended Rules vzde G.O.Rt.No.03 dt.05.02.2021. Therefore,, it is
clear that the Govemment intended to fill up the vacancies of School
Assistants including the upgraded posts of School Assistants
(Languages) and School Assistants (Physical Education) and has arrived
at the total number of vacancies in accordance with the Rules as

said G.Os. hare to be consiclered as held by the Hon'ble Apex Court in
thecaseofstateofHimachalPratleshandothersVs.RajKumar
and others (2 supra). If the Govemment did not intend to lill up the
vacancies, but the petitioners were sceking consideration of their cases
lor promotion to the vacancies in accordance with old Rules' the
reliance of the leamed (iovernment Pleader on the above judgment
would be relevant. But, since the vacancies were not frlled up due to
court orders and not because the Government did not want to fill up the

Government Pleader that the vacancies have to be filled up as per

Govemment initiated steps to filt up the vacancies and onll' after this
Court pointed out that they would have to be filled up as per Rules in
force and not without amending the Rules, they have resorted to
amendment of Rules and withdrawal of the vacancies and rescheduling
the panel and vacancies. This Court is of the opinion that by issuance of
